I mean, I’m not trying to dodge the question… I just haven’t really been tracking it that way.\n\nI guess I’ve been kind of flat or worn down a lot of the time, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds pretty strong. I’m not sure. Is that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say that clearly. There have definitely been days where I felt kind of low or heavy, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds like a lot, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been off more than usual, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s been that constant. Is that still useful?\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the past two weeks, would you say there were more than half of the days when you felt down or low most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I honestly don’t know. More than half sounds like something I should be able to answer, but I haven’t been marking the days or anything.\n\nThere were some days I felt pretty low for a good stretch, yeah. But I’m not sure I can say it was most of the time on more than half the days. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be agreeable. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past two weeks, on the days when you felt low or heavy, would you say that feeling lasted for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. On those days, it could hang around for a while, like a good chunk of the day, but I don’t know if I’d say most of the day every time.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Maybe some days it did, but I wasn’t really watching the clock on it. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: On the days when you felt low or heavy, would you say that your mood was depressed or sad rather than just feeling tired or unmotivated?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to separate those, to be honest. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that. Sometimes it just feels… heavy, kind of flat.\n\nI guess there are moments where it feels more like sadness than just being tired, but I don’t know if I’d call it full-on depressed. I’m trying to answer it the right way—I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt a lack of energy or felt run down for most of the day on most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but mostly I just don’t feel like myself anymore.\n\nIt’s not only being tired, exactly. I used to hike, coach, play guitar, call people back… normal stuff. Lately I just don’t really do those things. Some days I’m in that small rental for hours, and I know there are things I should do, but I just can’t seem to get myself started.\n\nSo… I guess yes, run down or no energy is probably fair. Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you completely lost interest in doing things you used to enjoy, like hiking, coaching, or playing guitar, even for a little while?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have, mostly. I don’t know if that’s the sort of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things as much as you used to, like you don’t get the same satisfaction out of hiking, coaching, or playing guitar anymore?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but those things just don’t really pull me in anymore. Hiking, coaching, guitar… I used to get something out of them, and now I mostly just don’t start.\n\nSome days I’m just sitting in that little rental for hours, knowing I should do something, call someone back or pick up the guitar, and I just… can’t seem to get myself moving. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I’m not sure. Is that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you had days where you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say that clearly. There have definitely been days where I felt kind of low or heavy, but “most of the day, nearly every day” sounds like a lot, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nI guess I’d say I’ve been off more than usual, but I haven’t really kept track of whether it’s been that constant. Is that still useful?\n\nDoctor: Looking back at the past two weeks, would you say there were more than half of the days when you felt down or low most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I honestly don’t know. More than half sounds like something I should be able to answer, but I haven’t been marking the days or anything.\n\nThere were some days I felt pretty low for a good stretch, yeah. But I’m not sure I can say it was most of the time on more than half the days. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be agreeable. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past two weeks, on the days when you felt low or heavy, would you say that feeling lasted for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: On the days when you felt low or heavy, would you say that your mood was depressed or sad rather than just feeling tired or unmotivated?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to separate those, to be honest. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything like that. I’m trying to answer it the right way—I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt a lack of energy or felt run down for most of the day on most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
If you count “even part of the day,” then… yeah, I guess maybe it could be five days. Not five whole days, but there were probably at least that many where I felt down for a while.
